<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def_substringedit}
Let $\Sigma$ be an alphabet and $\bmx,\bmy\in\Sigma^*$. We say that $\bmy$ is obtained from $\bmx$ by a \emph{$k$-substring edit} if
\begin{equation}\label{eq_editbasic}
 \bmx=\bmu\bms\bmv,
 \quad
 \bmy=\bmu\bmt\bmv,
\end{equation}
for some sequences $\bmu,\bms,\bmt,\bmv\in\Sigma^{*}$ with $ \left|\bms\right|, \left|\bmt\right|\le k$ and $ \left|\bms\right|+ \left|\bmt\right|>0$.
In other words, the edit replaces $\bms$ by $\bmt$. We write $\bmx\to_k\bmy$ whenever such a representation exists, and call the edit \emph{nontrivial} if
$\bmx\ne\bmy$.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
A nonempty set $\cC\subseteq\Sigma_q^n$ is a
\emph{$(b,k)$-substring-edit correcting code} if
$\cE_{b,k}(\bmx)\cap\cE_{b,k}(\bmy)=\varnothing$ for all distinct $\bmx,\bmy\in\cC$.  When $b=1$, we simply call $\cC$ a \emph{$k$-substring-edit correcting code}.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
Let $q\ge2$, $b\ge1$, and $1\le k\le n$ be integers. Set $\tau=\min\left\{b,\left\lfloor n/k \right\rfloor\right\}$ and
    \begin{equation*}
        V_{q,k,b}(n)=\sum_{j=0}^{\tau}\binom{n-(k-1)j}{j}\left( (q-1)q^{k-1} \right)^j.
    \end{equation*}
    Let $\cC\subseteq\Sigma_q^n$ be a $(b,k)$-substring edit correcting code with maximum size. Then 
    \begin{equation}\label{eq_uplowbound}
        \frac{q^{n-2kb}}{(n+kb)^{2b}(k+1)^{4b}}\le \left|\cC\right|\le\min\left\{q^{\max\left\{0,n-2kb\right\}},\frac{q^n}{V_{q,k,b}(n)}\right\}.
    \end{equation}
    Equivalently, it holds that
    \begin{equation*}
       \max\left\{\min\left\{n,2kb\right\}\log q,\log V_{q,k,b}(n)\right\}\le\mathsf{red}_2(\cC)\le 2b\log(n+kb)+4b\log(k+1)+2kb\log q.
    \end{equation*}
    Suppose that $q$ and $b$ are fixed. When $n\ge2kb$, we have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq_lowredfixedqandb}
        \mathsf{red}_2(\cC)\ge\max\left\{2kb\log q,b\log n+kb\log q\right\}-O_{q,b}(1).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-1: body_md -->
The lower bound in \eqref{eq_uplowbound} is given in \cite[Lemma 2]{YuanyuanTang2025TCOMM}. We only have to prove the upper bound.

We first prove the first upper bound in \eqref{eq_uplowbound}. Put $m=\min\left\{n,2kb\right\}$, and fix an interval $I=[i,i+m-1]\subseteq[n]$. Let
$r=\lceil m/k\rceil$. Then $r\le2b$. For $j\in[r-1]$, denote $I_j=\left[ i+(j-1)k,i+jk-1 \right]$. Denote $I_r=\left[ i+(r-1)k,i+m-1 \right]$. Clearly, $I_1,\ldots,I_r$ are sub-intervals of $I$ of length at most $k$. Moreover, they form a partition of $I$.

Suppose that codewords $\bmx,\bmy\in\cC$ agree outside $I$, i.e., $\bmx_{[n]\setminus I}=\bmy_{[n]\setminus I}$. Form $\bmz\in\Sigma_q^n$ as follows:
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{array}{c}
       \bmz_{[n]\setminus I}=\bmx_{[n]\setminus I}=\bmy_{[n]\setminus I},\\
        \bmz_{I_j}=\bmx_{I_j}\text{ for }j\le\left\lfloor r/2 \right\rfloor,\\
        \bmz_{I_j}=\bmy_{I_j}\text{ for }j>\left\lfloor r/2 \right\rfloor.
    \end{array}
\end{equation*}
Since $r\le 2b$, we have $\left\lfloor r/2 \right\rfloor, r-\left\lfloor r/2 \right\rfloor\le b$. Then it follows that $\bmz\in\cE_{b,k}(\bmx)\cap\cE_{b,k}(\bmy)$. This implies $\bmx=\bmy$ because $\cC$ is a $(b,k)$-substring-edit correcting code. Therefore, it holds that $\bmx_{[n]\setminus I}\ne\bmy_{[n]\setminus I}$ for any distinct $\bmx,\bmy\in\cC$, which gives
$ \left|\cC\right|\le q^{n-m}=q^{\max\left\{n-2kb,0\right\}}$.

We next prove the second upper bound. Since $\cC$ is a $(b,k)$-substring-edit correcting code, by the sphere-packing argument, it is sufficient to establish the following lower bound on the size of $\cE_{b,k}(\bmx)\cap\Sigma_q^n$.
\begin{clm}
    For every $\bmx\in\Sigma_q^n$, it holds that $ \left|\cE_{b,k}(\bmx)\cap\Sigma_q^n\right|\ge V_{q,k,b}(n)$.
\end{clm}
\noindent\textit{Proof of Claim:}
It is clear that $\bmx\in\cE_{b,k}(\bmx)\cap\Sigma_q^n$. We next estimate the size of $\cE_{b,k}(\bmx)\cap\Sigma_q^n\setminus\{\bmx\}$.

For each integer $j\in[\tau]$, choose $j$ pairwise disjoint intervals
\begin{equation}\label{eq_constraintofI}
 I_h=[i_h,i_h+k-1],\quad
 1\le i_1<\cdots<i_j\le n-k+1,
 \quad i_{h+1}\ge i_h+k\quad(1\le h<j).
\end{equation}
For each choice of the tuple $\left( I_1,\ldots,I_j \right)$, define $j+1$ integers $a_1,a_2,\ldots,a_{j+1}$ as follows:
\begin{equation}\label{eq_itoa}
    \begin{array}{c}
        a_1=i_1-1,\\
        a_h=i_h-i_{h-1},\text{ for every }2\le h\le j,\\
        a_{j+1}=n+1-i_j.
    \end{array}
\end{equation}
Clearly, the tuple $\left( a_1,\ldots,a_{j+1} \right)$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq_constraintofa}
   \begin{array}{c}
        a_1+a_2+\cdots+a_{j+1}=n,\\
        a_1\ge0,\\
        a_h\ge k,\text{ for every }2\le h\le j+1.
   \end{array}
\end{equation}
On the other hand, it follows from \eqref{eq_itoa} that each tuple $\left( a_1,\ldots,a_{j+1} \right)\in\mathbb{Z}^{j+1}$ satisfying \eqref{eq_constraintofa} uniquely determines a tuple $\left( I_1,\ldots,I_j \right)$ satisfying \eqref{eq_constraintofI}. Therefore, for each $j\in[\tau]$, the number of the tuples $\left( I_1,\ldots,I_j \right)$ satisfying \eqref{eq_constraintofI} equals the number of tuples $\left( a_1,\ldots,a_{j+1} \right)\in\mathbb{Z}^{j+1}$ satisfying \eqref{eq_constraintofa}, which is
$\binom{n-(k-1)j}{j}$. 

For every $j\in[\tau]$ and every tuple $\left( I_1,\ldots,I_j \right)$ that satisfies \eqref{eq_constraintofI}, form a sequence $\bmz\in\Sigma_q^n$ from $\bmx$ as follows: on each $I_h$, replace $\bmx_{I_h}$ with an arbitrary $\bmt^{(h)}\in\Sigma_q^k$ satisfying $t^{(h)}_1\ne x_{i_h}$. There are $(q-1)q^{k-1}$ choices for each replacement. By construction, sequences $\bmz$ and $\bmx$ differ at at least $j$ coordinates.

Distinct tuples $\left( j,I_1,\ldots,I_j,\bmt^{(1)},\ldots,\bmt^{(j)} \right)$ produce distinct $\bmz$. Indeed, for an output $\bmz$,
let $D(\bmx,\bmz)=\left\{i\in[n]:x_i\ne z_i\right\}$, which is not empty because $\bmx\ne\bmz$. Then $i_1=\min D(\bmx,\bmz)$ and thus, the interval $I_1$ is determined. After removing the elements of
$D(\bmx,\bmz)$ that lie in $I_1$, the smallest remaining element, if one exists, is $i_2$. This gives $I_2$.  Repeating this procedure recovers $j$ and all the intervals $I_1,\ldots,I_j$. Finally, we have $\left( \bmt^{(1)},\ldots,\bmt^{(j)} \right)=\left( \bmz_{I_1},\ldots,\bmz_{I_j} \right)$.

The preceding argument shows that $ \left|\cE_{b,k}(\bmx)\cap\Sigma_q^n\setminus\{\bmx\}\right|$ is at least the number of tuples $\left( j,I_1,\ldots,I_j,\bmt^{(1)},\ldots,\bmt^{(j)} \right)$, which is $\sum_{j=1}^{\tau}\binom{n-(k-1)j}{j}\left( (q-1)q^{k-1} \right)^j=V_{q,k,b}(n)-1$. This completes the proof of the claim.
\hfill$\square$

Finally, if $n\ge 2kb$, the term with $j=b$ gives
\begin{align*}
 V_{q,k,b}(n)&\ge
 \binom{n-(k-1)b}{b}\left( (q-1)q^{k-1} \right)^{b}\\
 &\ge\left( \frac{n-(k-1)b}{b} \right)^b\left( (q-1)q^{k-1} \right)^b\\
 &\ge\left( \frac{n}{2b} \right)^b\left( (q-1)q^{k-1} \right)^b.
\end{align*}
Now \eqref{eq_lowredfixedqandb} follows.
<!-- end ieeeproof-1 -->
<!-- clm-1: body_md -->
For every $\bmx\in\Sigma_q^n$, it holds that $ \left|\cE_{b,k}(\bmx)\cap\Sigma_q^n\right|\ge V_{q,k,b}(n)$.
<!-- end clm-1 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\label{def_editregion}
    Let $\bmx$ and $\bmy$ be represented as in \eqref{eq_editbasic} and \eqref{eq_minimalends}. Set $i= \left|\bmu\right|$. If $ \left|\bms\right|>0$, the \emph{edit region} in $\bmx$ is the interval $[i+1,i+ \left|\bms\right|]$. If $ \left|\bms\right|=0$, it must be that $ \left|\bmt\right|>0$. In this case, the edit region in $\bmx$ is the \emph{gap} $g=i+1$ between $x_i$ and $x_{i+1}$, with the conventions that $g=1$ precedes the first symbol $x_1$ and $g= \left|\bmx\right|+1$ follows the last symbol $x_{ \left|\bmx\right|}$. Equivalently, if $ \left|\bms\right|>0$, the edit region is the coordinates occupied by $\bms$; if $ \left|\bms\right|=0$, the edit region is the position where $\bmt$ is inserted.
    
    The edit region in $\bmy$ is defined analogously using $\bmt$.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\label{def_covereditregion}
    For a coordinate interval $J=[a,b]\subseteq[1, \left|\bmx\right|]$, we say that $J$ or $\bmx_J$ \emph{covers} the edit region in $\bmx$ if $[i+1,i+ \left|\bms\right|]\subseteq J$ when $ \left|\bms\right|>0$, and if $a\le i+1\le b+1$ when $ \left|\bms\right|=0$. Coverage in $\bmy$ is defined in the same way.
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
Here we give some examples to illustrate \Cref{def_editregion,def_covereditregion}. Each edit region refers to the specified decomposition in \eqref{eq_editbasic}.
\begin{enumerate}[$(i)$]
\item Let $\bmu=01$, $\bms=10$, $\bmt=01$, and $\bmv=01$. Replacing $\bms$ by $\bmt$ changes $\bmx=011001$ to $\bmy=010101$. Both edit regions are $[3,4]$. The interval $[2,5]$ covers either region, whereas $[3,3]$ does not.
\item Let $\bmu=01$, $\bms=10$, $\bmt=\epsilon$, and $\bmv=01$. Deleting $\bms$ changes $\bmx=011001$ to $\bmy=0101$. The edit region in $\bmx$ is $[3,4]$, and the edit region in $\bmy$ is the gap $g=3$. In $\bmy$, each of $[1,2]$, $[3,4]$ and $[2,3]$ covers this gap. The interval $[1,1]$ does not cover it.
\item Reversing the edit in (ii) inserts $10$ into $\bmx=0101$ and gives $\bmy=011001$. The edit region in $\bmx$ is now the gap $g=3$, and that in $\bmy$ is $[3,4]$.
\item For $\bmx=01$, insertion before the first symbol has gap $g=1$, covered by $[1,1]$. Insertion after the last symbol has gap $g=3$, covered by $[2,2]$. Thus endpoint insertions are included in \Cref{def_covereditregion}.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\cite[Theorem 3.3]{Shoup2008}\label{prop_bitarithmetic}
Let $a,b\in\mathbb Z$. Then the following computations can be performed deterministically.
\begin{enumerate}[$(i)$]
\item Addition and subtraction, $a\pm b$, require $O\left( \operatorname{len}(a)+\operatorname{len}(b) \right)$ bit operations.
\item Multiplication, $ab$, requires $O\left( \operatorname{len}(a)\operatorname{len}(b) \right)$ bit operations.
\item If $b>0$, the quotient $c=\left\lfloor a/b \right\rfloor$ and the remainder $r=a-bc\in[0,b-1]$ can be computed in $O\left( \operatorname{len}(b)\operatorname{len}(c) \right)$ bit operations. In particular, this is $O\left( \operatorname{len}(a)\operatorname{len}(b) \right)$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- assumption-1: body_md -->
\label{assump1}
    There exist integers $p_1,p_2\ge0$ and $1\le l_1,l_2\le K$ such that
 \begin{equation*}
 \begin{aligned}
  \Part_{\cP}(\bmx)
   &=\left( \bme_1,\ldots,\bme_{p_1},
            \bma_1,\ldots,\bma_{l_1},
            \bmh_1,\ldots,\bmh_{p_2} \right),\\
  \Part_{\cP}(\bmy)
   &=\left( \bme_1,\ldots,\bme_{p_1},
            \bmb_1,\ldots,\bmb_{l_2},
            \bmh_1,\ldots,\bmh_{p_2} \right).
 \end{aligned}
 \end{equation*}
 The edit region in $\bmx$ is covered by the substring
 $\bma_1\cdots\bma_{l_1}$, and the edit region in $\bmy$ is covered by the substring $\bmb_1\cdots\bmb_{l_2}$.
<!-- end assumption-1 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop_equallength}
    Let $d$, $E$ and $\Delta_{\rm VT}$ be defined in \eqref{eq_dEDelta}. Suppose that $d=0$, and that $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ differ in one coordinate or two consecutive coordinates. For $0\le r\le 2$, set $c_r=G_r(\bmz)-G_r(\bmw)$. If $E\ne0$, and all the values of $E$, $\Delta_{\rm VT}$, $c_0$ and $c_1$ are known, we can determine exactly the first index at which $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ differ.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-2: body_md -->
It is clear that $c_0+c_1+c_2=E$. Thus, we can obtain $c_2$ when $c_0$, $c_1$ and $E$ are given. Since $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ differ in one or two consecutive coordinates, exactly one or two of $c_0$, $c_1$ and $c_2$ are nonzero.

    If only one of $c_0$, $c_1$ and $c_2$ is nonzero, there is a unique $j$ such that $z_j\ne w_j$. The definitions of $E$ and $\Delta_{\rm VT}$ give $E=z_j-w_j$ and $\Delta_{\rm VT}=j(z_j-w_j)$. Therefore, $j=\Delta_{\rm VT}/E$.

    Now suppose that exactly two of $c_0$, $c_1$ and $c_2$ are nonzero. Then there are two indices $j$ and $j+1$ at which $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ differ. Let $r$ be the unique element of $\{0,1,2\}$ satisfying $c_r=0$. It follows that $j\equiv r+1\pmod{3}$ and $z_{j+1}-w_{j+1}=c_{(r+2)\bmod 3}$. Moreover, 
    \begin{align*}
        \Delta_{\rm VT}&=j(z_j-w_j)+(j+1)\left( z_{j+1}-w_{j+1} \right)\\
        &=j\left( z_j-w_j+z_{j+1}-w_{j+1} \right)+z_{j+1}-w_{j+1}\\
        &=j\cdot E+z_{j+1}-w_{j+1}.
    \end{align*}
    Consequently, it holds that $j=\left( \Delta_{\rm VT}-c_{(r+2)\bmod 3} \right)/E$. This completes the proof.
<!-- end ieeeproof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem_monotonicity}
    If $d\ne0$ and $ \left|E\right|\le w_i$ for all $i\in\left[  \left|\bmw\right| \right]$, the function $\eta(r)$ is monotone in $r$.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-3: body_md -->
By definition, for $r,r-1\in\left[ 0, \left|\bmw\right| \right]$, it holds that $\eta(r)-\eta(r-1)=d\cdot w_r+E$. Since $d$ is a non-zero integer and $ \left|E\right|\le w_i$ for all $i\in\left[  \left|\bmw\right| \right]$, we have $\eta(r)-\eta(r-1)\ge 0$ if $d>0$ and $\eta(r)-\eta(r-1)\le 0$ if $d<0$.
<!-- end ieeeproof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem_Deltaetagap}
    Let $\Delta_{\rm VT}$ be defined in \eqref{eq_dEDelta}, and $r^{*}$ and $\eta(\cdot)$ be defined in \eqref{eq_reta}. If both $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ are $A$-bounded, then $ \left|\Delta_{\rm VT}-\eta(r^{*})\right|<2K^2A$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-4: body_md -->
It follows from \eqref{eq_splitzw} that $\bmz_{\left[ 1,r^{*} \right]}=\bmw_{\left[ 1,r^{*} \right]}=\bmu^\prime$, $\bmz_{\left[ r^{*}+ \left|\bms^\prime\right|+1, \left|\bmz\right| \right]}=\bmw_{\left[ r^{*}+ \left|\bmt^\prime\right|+1, \left|\bmw\right| \right]}=\bmv^\prime$, $\bmz_{\left[ r^{*}+1,r^{*}+ \left|\bms^\prime\right| \right]}=\bms^\prime$ and $\bmw_{\left[ r^{*}+1,r^{*}+ \left|\bmt^\prime\right| \right]}=\bmt^\prime$. This implies that $d= \left|\bmw\right|- \left|\bmz\right|= \left|\bmt^\prime\right|- \left|\bms^\prime\right|$ and $E=\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmz\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)=\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bms^\prime\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmt^\prime\right)$. Therefore, we have
    \begin{align}
            \Delta_{\rm VT}&=\mathsf{VT}(\bmz)-\mathsf{VT}(\bmw)\notag\\
            &=\sum_{i=r^{*}+1}^{r^{*}+ \left|\bms^\prime\right|}iz_i+\sum_{i=r^{*}+ \left|\bms^\prime\right|+1}^{ \left|\bmz\right|}iz_i-\sum_{i=r^{*}+1}^{r^{*}+ \left|\bmt^\prime\right|}iw_i-\sum_{i=r^{*}+ \left|\bmt^\prime\right|+1}^{ \left|\bmw\right|}iw_i\notag\\
            &=\left( \sum_{i=r^{*}+1}^{r^{*}+ \left|\bms^\prime\right|}iz_i-\sum_{i=r^{*}+1}^{r^{*}+ \left|\bmt^\prime\right|}iw_i \right)+\left( \sum_{i=r^{*}+ \left|\bms^\prime\right|+1}^{ \left|\bmz\right|}iz_i-\sum_{i=r^{*}+ \left|\bmt^\prime\right|+1}^{ \left|\bmw\right|}iw_i \right)\notag\\
            &=\mathsf{VT}(\bms^\prime)-\mathsf{VT}(\bmt^\prime)+r^{*}\left( \mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bms^\prime\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmt^\prime\right) \right)-\left(  \left|\bmt^\prime\right|- \left|\bms^\prime\right| \right)\sum_{i=r^{*}+ \left|\bmt^\prime\right|+1}^{ \left|\bmw\right|}w_i\label{eq_VT}\\
            &=-d\sum_{i=r^{*}+1}^{ \left|\bmw\right|}w_i+r^{*}\cdot E+\mathsf{VT}(\bms^\prime)-\mathsf{VT}(\bmt^\prime)+\left(  \left|\bmt^\prime\right|- \left|\bms^\prime\right| \right)\sum_{i=r^{*}+1}^{r^{*}+ \left|\bmt^\prime\right|}w_i\notag\\
            &=\eta\left( r^{*} \right)+\mathsf{VT}(\bms^\prime)-\mathsf{VT}(\bmt^\prime)+\left(  \left|\bmt^\prime\right|- \left|\bms^\prime\right| \right)\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmt^\prime\right).\notag
    \end{align}
    Since both $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ are $A$-bounded, $K\ge2$, and $ \left|\bms^\prime\right|, \left|\bmt^\prime\right|\le K$, it holds that $ \left|\mathsf{VT}(\bms^\prime)-\mathsf{VT}(\bmt^\prime)\right|<K^2A$, $ \left| \left|\bmt^\prime\right|- \left|\bms^\prime\right|\right|\le K$ and $\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmt^\prime\right)\le KA$. Then it follows that $ \left|\Delta_{\rm VT}-\eta(r^{*})\right|\le \left|\mathsf{VT}(\bms^\prime)-\mathsf{VT}(\bmt^\prime)\right|+ \left| \left|\bmt^\prime\right|- \left|\bms^\prime\right|\right|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmt^\prime\right)<2K^2A$.
<!-- end ieeeproof-4 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop_unequallength}
    Suppose that $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ are $A$-bounded. Let $d$, $E$, $\Delta_{\rm VT}$ be defined by \eqref{eq_dEDelta}. Assume that $d\ne0$ and $ \left|E\right|\le w_i$ for every $i\in[ \left|\bmw\right|]$. Let $\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_{ \left|\bmw\right|}$ be positive numbers. Suppose that there are positive numbers $C^{(1)}$, $C^{(2)}$ and $C^{(3)}$ satisfying:
    \begin{enumerate}[$(i)$]
        \item $w_i\ge C^{(1)}\ell_i$ for every $i\in[ \left|\bmw\right|]$; 
        \item $\ell_i\ge C^{(2)}$ for every $i\in[ \left|\bmw\right|]$; 
        \item $ \left|E\right|\le C^{(3)}$;
        \item $C^{(3)}<C^{(1)}C^{(2)}$.
    \end{enumerate}
    Let $r^{*}$ and $\eta(\cdot)$ be defined as in \eqref{eq_reta}, $\cR=\left\{r\in\left[ 0, \left|\bmw\right| \right]: \left|\Delta_{\rm VT}-\eta(r)\right|<2K^2A\right\}$, $r_1=\min\cR$ and $r_2=\max\cR$. Then $r^{*}\in\cR$, $\cR=[r_1,r_2]$, and
    \begin{equation}\label{eq_sumupper}
        \sum_{i=r_1+1}^{r_2}\ell_i<\frac{4K^2A}{C^{(1)}-\frac{C^{(3)}}{C^{(2)}}}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-5: body_md -->
It follows from \Cref{lem_Deltaetagap} that $r^{*}\in\cR$. By \Cref{lem_monotonicity}, the function $\eta(r)$ is monotone in $r\in\left[ 0, \left|\bmw\right| \right]$. This implies $\cR=[r_1,r_2]$. Since $r_1,r_2\in\cR$, we have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq_diffupper}
         \left|\eta(r_2)-\eta(r_1)\right|<4K^2A.
    \end{equation}
    By the definition of $\eta(\cdot)$, it holds that $\eta(r)-\eta(r-1)=d\cdot w_r+E$ whenever $r,r-1\in\left[ 0, \left|\bmw\right| \right]$, which gives
    \begin{equation}\label{eq_lowstep}
        \begin{aligned}
             \left|\eta(r)-\eta(r-1)\right|&\ge  \left|d\right|w_r- \left|E\right|\\
            &\overset{(a)}{\ge} w_r- \left|E\right|\\
            &\overset{(b)}{\ge} C^{(1)}\ell_r-C^{(3)}\\
            &=\left( C^{(1)}-\frac{C^{(3)}}{\ell_r} \right)\ell_r\\
            &\overset{(c)}{\ge} \left( C^{(1)}-\frac{C^{(3)}}{C^{(2)}} \right)\ell_r,
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    where (a) uses the assumption $d\ne0$, (b) follows from conditions (i) and (iii), and (c) follows from condition (ii). Since $\eta(r)$ is monotone in $r$, we conclude from \eqref{eq_lowstep} that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq_difflower}
         \left|\eta(r_2)-\eta(r_1)\right|= \left|\sum_{r=r_1+1}^{r_2}\left( \eta(r)-\eta(r-1) \right)\right|=\sum_{r=r_1+1}^{r_2} \left|\eta(r)-\eta(r-1)\right|\ge \left( C^{(1)}-\frac{C^{(3)}}{C^{(2)}} \right)\sum_{r=r_1+1}^{r_2}\ell_r.
    \end{equation}
    Now \eqref{eq_sumupper} follows from \eqref{eq_diffupper}, \eqref{eq_difflower} and condition (iv).
<!-- end ieeeproof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem_absVT}
    Let $\bmz\in\mathbb{Z}_{\ge0}^{*}$ and $\bmz\to_{K}\bmw$ for some $K\ge2$. Suppose that both $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ are $A$-bounded, and that $ \left|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmz\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)\right|\le w_i$ for all $i\in[ \left|\bmw\right|]$. Then $ \left|\Delta_{\rm VT}\right|<K^2\left( \mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmz\right)+A \right)$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-6: body_md -->
Represent $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ as in \eqref{eq_splitzw}. Since $\bmw_{\left[ r^{*}+ \left|\bmt^\prime\right|+1, \left|\bmw\right| \right]}=\bmv^\prime$, we conclude from \eqref{eq_VT} that 
    \begin{align*}
     \left|\Delta_{\rm VT}\right|&\le \left|\mathsf{VT}(\bms^\prime)-\mathsf{VT}(\bmt^\prime)\right|+ \left|\bmu^\prime\right| \left|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bms^\prime\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmt^\prime\right)\right|+ \left| \left|\bmt^\prime\right|- \left|\bms^\prime\right|\right|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmv^\prime\right)\\
    &<K^2A+ \left|\bmu^\prime\right| \left|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmz\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)\right|+ \left| \left|\bmt^\prime\right|- \left|\bms^\prime\right|\right|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmv^\prime\right)\\
    &\le K^2A+\sum_{i=1}^{ \left|\bmu^\prime\right|} \left|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmz\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)\right|+K\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmv^\prime\right)\\
    &\le K^2A+\sum_{i=1}^{ \left|\bmu^\prime\right|}w_i+K\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmv^\prime\right)\\
    &\overset{(a)}{=}K^2A+\sum_{i=1}^{ \left|\bmu^\prime\right|}z_i+K\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmv^\prime\right)\\
    &\le K^2A+\sum_{i=1}^{ \left|\bmu^\prime\right|}z_i+K\sum_{i= \left|\bmu^\prime\right|+1}^{ \left|\bmz\right|}z_i\\
    &\le K^2\left( \mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmz\right)+A \right),
    \end{align*}
    where (a) follows from the fact that $z_i=w_i$ for every $1\le i\le  \left|\bmu^\prime\right|$.
<!-- end ieeeproof-6 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
\label{def_locatable}
Let $\bmz,\bmw\in\mathbb{Z}_{\ge0}^{*}$. Suppose that $\bmw$ is obtained from $\bmz$ by a nontrivial $K$-substring edit. We call this $K$-substring edit \emph{strongly locatable} if the following three conditions are satisfied:
\begin{enumerate}[$(i)$]
\item $ \left|\bmz\right|\ne \left|\bmw\right|$ or $\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmz\right)\ne\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)$;
\item if $ \left|\bmz\right|= \left|\bmw\right|$, then $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ differ in one coordinate or two consecutive coordinates;
\item $|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmz\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)|< w_i$ for every $i\in\left[  \left|\bmw\right| \right]$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
Li \emph{et al.} introduced the definition of a \emph{locatable} substring edit in \cite[Definition~3]{YutingLi2025IT}, which satisfies only conditions (i) and (iii) in \Cref{def_locatable}. We additionally include condition (ii) in the definition. This is the first reason why we call the edit \emph{strongly} locatable. The second reason is that while the locatability property helps to determine the edit $\bmx\to_{k}\bmy$ to within a substring of $\bmx$ of length $O_{q,k}\left( (\log n)^2 \right)$ (see Lemma 4 and the proof of Theorem 19 in \cite{YutingLi2025IT}), the strong locatability property enables us to determine the edit $\bmx\to_{k}\bmy$ to within a substring of $\bmx$ of length $O_{q,k}(\log n)$ (see \Cref{thm_framework} and \Cref{thm_specificlocator} in this paper).
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm_framework}
    Let $\bmx\in\Sigma_q^n$, where $n>k$, and let $\bmy$ be obtained from $\bmx$ by a nontrivial $k$-substring edit. Let $\cP$ be a partition rule and $f:\Sigma_q^{*}\to\mathbb{Z}_{\ge0}$ be a function. Set $\bmz=\bmF_{\cP}(\bmx)$ and $\bmw=\bmF_{\cP}(\bmy)$. Suppose that the following conditions hold.
    \begin{enumerate}[$(i)$]
        \item \Cref{assump1} holds.
        \item The two sequences $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ are $A$-bounded, where $A\ge1$ is an integer.
        \item The $K$-substring edit $\bmz\to_{K}\bmw$ is strongly locatable, where $K\ge2$ is an integer.
        \item Each segment of $\bmy$ under $\cP$ has length at most $L$.
        \item The values of $A$, $K$ and $L$ are known.
        \item There are known positive numbers $C^{(1)},C^{(2)},C^{(3)}$ such that if $d\ne 0$,  they satisfy, for $\ell_i=|\bmy_i^{\cP}|$ and every $i\in[ \left|\bmw\right|]$, $w_i\ge C^{(1)}\ell_i$, $\ell_i\ge C^{(2)}$, and $ \left|E\right|\le C^{(3)}<C^{(1)}C^{(2)}$, where $d$ and $E$ are defined in \eqref{eq_dEDelta}.
    \end{enumerate} 
    Let $G_0(\cdot)$, $G_1(\cdot)$ and $\Delta_{\rm VT}$ be defined as before. Suppose that $d$, $E$, $\Delta_{\rm VT}$, together with $G_0(\bmz)-G_0(\bmw)$ and $G_1(\bmz)-G_1(\bmw)$ when $d=0$, are given. Then one can determine from $\bmy$ an interval $I\subseteq[n]$ that covers the edit region in $\bmx$ and satisfies
    \begin{equation}\label{eq_geninterval}
     \left|I\right|\le\max\left\{(2K-1)L,\frac{4K^2A}{C^{(1)}-\frac{C^{(3)}}{C^{(2)}}}+KL\right\}+2k.
    \end{equation}
    Given $\Part_{\cP}(\bmy)$ and $\bmw$, this interval can be found using $O_{q,k}\left( n\lambda^2 \right)$ bit operations, where $\lambda=\left\lceil \log\left( (n+k+1)^2K^2A \right) \right\rceil$.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-7: body_md -->
The preceding analysis shows how to find such an interval. We only need to count bit operations. Since $\bmz\to_K\bmw$, we have $|d|\le K$. Since every segment is nonempty, we obtain $ \left|\bmz\right|\le n$ and $ \left|\bmw\right|\le n+k$. Then the $A$-bounded property of $\bmz$ and $\bmw$ gives $ \left|E\right|\le KA$, $ \left|\Delta_{\rm VT}\right|\le A(n+k+1)^2$, and $ \left|G_r(\bmz)-G_r(\bmw)\right|\le A(n+k)$ for every $0\le r\le 2$. Each value of $\eta(r)$ has absolute value at most $2KA(n+k)$. We need to compare each $\eta(r)$ with $\Delta_{\rm VT}-2K^2A$ and $\Delta_{\rm VT}+2K^2A$. Thus all integers used below, including $2K^2A$, $\Delta_{\rm VT}-\eta(r)$, segment indices, and coordinate sums, have $O(\lambda)$ bits.

    If $d=0$, the proof of \Cref{prop_equallength} computes the index $j$ by a constant number of additions, subtractions, and comparisons, followed by one exact division by $E$. Hence it costs $O(\lambda^2)$ bit operations. Set $a_0=\max\left\{1,j-K+1\right\}$ and $b_0=\min\left\{ \left|\bmw\right|,j+K-1\right\}$. Computing these two indices costs $O(\lambda)$ further bit operations.

    Now suppose that $d\ne0$. Compute the threshold $2K^2A$ and initialize $\eta\left(  \left|\bmw\right| \right)= \left|\bmw\right|E$. These computations cost $O(\lambda^2)$ bit operations. For $r= \left|\bmw\right|, \left|\bmw\right|-1,\ldots,1$, compute $\eta(r-1)=\eta(r)-dw_r-E$. At every index $r\in[0, \left|\bmw\right|]$, test whether $ \left|\Delta_{\rm VT}-\eta(r)\right|<2K^2A$. For each $r$, the process uses a fixed number of multiplications, additions, subtractions, and comparisons. Therefore, scanning all $ \left|\bmw\right|+1$ indices costs $O\left( (n+k+1)\lambda^2 \right)$ bit operations. Set $a_0=r_1+1$ and $b_0=\min\left\{ \left|\bmw\right|,r_2+K\right\}$, which costs $O(\lambda)$ further bit operations.

    In either case, the selected substring of $\bmy$ is $\bmy_{J}=\bmy_{a_0}^{\cP}\cdots\bmy_{b_0}^{\cP}$. Determining $J$ and $I$ requires additional $O(n+k)$ bit operations. Therefore, the total number of bit operations is $O_{q,k}\left( n\lambda^2 \right)$.
<!-- end ieeeproof-7 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
Before proceeding, we explain why we consider the partition of $\widehat{\bma}$ instead of $\bma$. Clearly, the sequence $\widehat{\bma}$ is obtained by adding a copy of $\bmp_{k}$ to both ends of $\bma$. Both copies are needed for our construction.  The left copy starts at coordinate~$1$.  Consequently every segment of $\widehat{\bma}$ begins with a complete occurrence of $\bmp_k$ and hence has length at least
$2k$. Without this copy, the first segment could have length in $[1,2k-1]$, so the inequality $\ell_i\ge C^{(2)}=2k$ used in \Cref{prop_unequallength,thm_framework} would be false.  In addition, for a $k$-substring edit before the first $\bmp_{k}$ inside $\bma$, the unchanged pattern start $\alpha$ chosen in the proof of \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement}~(ii) would not exist.

The right copy begins at coordinate $|\widehat{\bma}|-2k+1$. It guarantees an unchanged pattern occurrence to the right of every edit in $\bma$. Without it, a $k$-substring edit after the last $\bmp_{k}$ inside $\bma$ could leave no such occurrence, and the pattern start $\beta$ in the proof of \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement}~(ii) would not exist.  The decompositions in \eqref{eq_segmentreplacement}, and hence condition~(i) of \Cref{thm_framework}, would then require separate boundary cases and would not follow from the stated argument.

Thus the left and right
copies respectively supply the two pattern starts that bound the edit from the left and from the right. Omitting either copy breaks a specific hypothesis used by the present proof.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
\label{def_descrepattern}
A pattern in $\widehat{\bmx}$ with start $a\in\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)\triangleq\cT_k(\widehat{\bmx})\setminus\cU\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)$ is called a \emph{destroyed pattern}. In other words, the interval $[a,a+2k-1]$ intersects the edit region in $\widehat{\bmx}$ in the case $ \left|\bms\right|>0$, or crosses the edit region in $\widehat{\bmx}$ in the case $\bms=\epsilon$. 

A pattern in $\widehat{\bmy}$ with start $b\in\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)\triangleq\cT_k(\widehat{\bmy})\setminus\cU\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)$ is called a \emph{created pattern}. In other words, the interval $[b,b+2k-1]$ intersects the edit region in $\widehat{\bmy}$ in the case $ \left|\bmt\right|>0$, or crosses the edit region in $\widehat{\bmy}$ in the case $\bmt=\epsilon$.
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
Let $q=k=2$. Then $\bmp_k=0011$. Take $\bmu=00$, $\bms=0$, $\bmt=\epsilon$, and $\bmv=11$. This edit deletes the third symbol of $\bmx=00011$ and gives $\bmy=0011$. The extended sequences are $\widehat{\bmx}=0011\,00\,0\,11\,0011$ and $\widehat{\bmy}=0011\,00\,11\,0011$. Here $\rho=6$ and $d=-1$. By definition, we have
$$
\begin{aligned}
\cT_k(\widehat{\bmx})&=\{1,6,10\}, &\cU(\widehat{\bmx})&=\{1,10\}, &\cD_{\rm es}(\widehat{\bmx})=\{6\},\\
\cT_k(\widehat{\bmy})&=\{1,5,9\}, &\cU(\widehat{\bmy})&=\{1,9\}, &\cC_{\rm re}(\widehat{\bmy})=\{5\}.
\end{aligned}
$$
The destroyed pattern occupies $[6,9]$ and contains the deleted coordinate $7$. The created pattern occupies $[5,8]$ and crosses the edit region $g=7$ in $\widehat{\bmy}$.
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem_pdense}
For fixed integers $q\ge2$ and $k\ge1$, let $n\ge\max\left\{\ell_{n,k},q^{\frac{(6k+3)\log q-\log\mathsf{e}}{0.4\log q}}\right\}$ be an integer.
\begin{enumerate}[$(i)$]
    \item(\textit{cf.}~\cite[Lemma 6]{Song2025Entropy}) It holds that $ \left|\cD_{q,k}(n)\right|\ge q^{n-1}$.
    \item If $\bmx\in\cD_{q,k}(n)$, every segment of $\widehat{\bmx}$ has length at most $\ell_{n,k}$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-8: body_md -->
Claim~(i) follows from \cite[Lemma 6]{Song2025Entropy}, where Song \emph{et al.} presented an efficient algorithm that encodes any sequence in $\Sigma_q^{n-1}$ into $\cD_{q,k}(n)$ whenever $n\ge q^{\frac{(6k+3)\log q-\log\mathsf{e}}{0.4\log q}}$, where $\mathsf{e}$ is the base of the natural logarithm. Claim~(ii) follows immediately from the definition.
<!-- end ieeeproof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem_segmentreplacement}
Let $\bmx\in\Sigma_q^n$ and $\bmx\to_k\bmy$. Write $\widehat{\bmx}$ and $\widehat{\bmy}$ as in \eqref{eq_extendededit}.
\begin{enumerate}[$(i)$]
    \item It holds that $\bmF_{k}(\bmy)_i\ge C_{k} \left|\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_i\right|$ and $ \left|\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_i\right|\ge 2k$ for every $1\le i\le m_{k}(\widehat{\bmy})$. Thus, one may take $C^{(1)}=C_{k}$ and $C^{(2)}=2k$ in \Cref{prop_unequallength}.
    \item There are sequences of segments $\bmP$, $\bmS$, $\bmA$ and $\bmA^\prime$ such that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq_segmentreplacement}
    \mathsf{Part}_k\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)=\bmP\bmA\bmS,
    \;
    \mathsf{Part}_k\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)=\bmP\bmA^\prime\bmS.
    \end{equation}
    Let $r= \left|\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)\right|$ and $r^\prime= \left|\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)\right|$. Then $\bmA$ contains $r+1$ segments, and $\bmA^\prime$ contains $r^\prime+1$ segments. Moreover,
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $0\le r,r^\prime\le2$;
        \item the edit region in $\widehat{\bmx}$ is covered by the concatenation of segments in $\bmA$;
        \item the edit region in $\widehat{\bmy}$ is covered by the concatenation of segments in $\bmA^\prime$.
    \end{itemize}
    \item The sequence $\bmF_{k}(\bmy)$ is obtained from $\bmF_{k}(\bmx)$ by one $3$-substring edit, and $ \left|m_k(\bmx)-m_k(\bmy)\right|\le2$.
    \item  We have
          \begin{equation}\label{eq_Fsumdiff}
              \left|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmy)\right)\right|
             \le C_{k}(k+1)-1.
         \end{equation}
         Thus one may take $C^{(3)}=C_k(k+1)-1$ in
         \Cref{prop_unequallength}.
    \item If $\bmx\in\cD_{q,k}(n)$, every segment of $\widehat{\bmy}$ has length at most $3\ell_{n,k}+k$.
    \item If $\bmx\in\cD_{q,k}(n)$, both $\bmF_{k}(\bmx)$ and $\bmF_{k}(\bmy)$ are $A_{n,k}$ bounded, where
    \begin{equation}\label{eq_Amu}
        A_{n,k}\triangleq Q-1+B\left( 3\ell_{n,k}+1 \right)+C_{k}(3\ell_{n,k}+k).
    \end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-9: body_md -->
Claim (i) follows from \eqref{eq_fmu}, because $D_q$ and $N_k$ are nonnegative and every segment of $\widehat{\bmy}$ begins with the length-$2k$ pattern. In the following, denote $\rho=2k+ \left|\bmu\right|$.

Next, we prove claim (ii). Let $a\in\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)$. By the definition of $\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)$, either
$[a,a+2k-1]\cap[\rho+1,\rho+ \left|\bms\right|]\ne\varnothing$ when $ \left|\bms\right|>0$, or $a\le\rho<a+2k-1$ when $ \left|\bms\right|=0$. Both cases imply $a\in[\rho-2k+2,\rho+ \left|\bms\right|]$. This interval has length
$2k+ \left|\bms\right|-1\le3k-1$, whereas distinct pattern starts differ by at least $2k$. Therefore, the interval $[\rho-2k+2,\rho+ \left|\bms\right|]$ contains at most two starts, which implies $ \left|\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)\right|\le2$. Similarly, replacing
$(\widehat{\bmx},\bms,\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right))$ by
$(\widehat{\bmy},\bmt,\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right))$ gives $ \left|\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)\right|\le2$.

Let $\alpha$ be the largest start
in $\cU\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)$ satisfying $\alpha\le\rho-2k+1$, and let $\beta$ be the
smallest start in $\cU\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)$ satisfying
$\beta\ge\rho+ \left|\bms\right|+1$. Their existence follows from \eqref{eq_elementUxUy}. Write $\cU\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)\cap[\rho-2k+1]=\left\{i_1,\ldots,i_L\right\}$ and $\cU\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)\cap[\rho+ \left|\bms\right|+1, \left|\widehat{\bmx}\right|]=\left\{j_1,\ldots,j_R\right\}$, where $1=i_1<\cdots<i_L=\alpha$ and $\beta=j_1<\cdots<j_R=n+2k+1$. Then $\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ i_1,i_2-1 \right]},\ldots,\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ i_{L-1},i_L-1 \right]}$ are all the segments of $\widehat{\bmx}$ whose starts are to the left of $\alpha$, and $\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ j_1,j_2-1 \right]},\ldots,\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ j_{R-1},j_R-1 \right]},\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ j_{R}, \left|\widehat{\bmx}\right| \right]}$ are all the segments of $\widehat{\bmx}$ whose starts are no smaller than $\beta$. Let $\bmP=\left( \widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ i_1,i_2-1 \right]},\ldots,\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ i_{L-1},i_L-1 \right]} \right)$ and $\bmS=\left( \widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ j_1,j_2-1 \right]},\ldots,\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ j_{R-1},j_R-1 \right]},\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ j_{R}, \left|\widehat{\bmx}\right| \right]} \right)$. Notice that $\bmP$ might be empty.

By the definition of $\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)$, the set $\left\{\alpha\right\}\cup\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)$ consists of the pattern starts that determine all the segments of $\widehat{\bmx}$ which do not appear in $\bmP$ or $\bmS$. Let $\bmA$ be the sequence of these $r+1$ segments. Then we have $\Part_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)=\bmP\bmA\bmS$.

By \eqref{eq_correspondxy}, $\alpha$ is also the largest start in $\cU\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)$ satisfying $\alpha\le\rho-2k+1$, and $\phi(\beta)$ is the
smallest start in $\cU\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)$ satisfying
$\phi(\beta)\ge\rho+ \left|\bmt\right|+1$. Then it follows from \eqref{eq_correspondxy} and \eqref{eq_Uleft} that $\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ i_1,i_2-1 \right]},\ldots,\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ i_{L-1},i_L-1 \right]}$ are all the segments of $\widehat{\bmy}$ whose starts are to the left of $\alpha$, and that $\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ j_1,j_2-1 \right]},\ldots,\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ j_{R-1},j_R-1 \right]},\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ j_{R}, \left|\widehat{\bmx}\right| \right]}$ are all the segments of $\widehat{\bmy}$ whose starts are no smaller than $\phi(\beta)$. By the definition of $\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)$, the set $\left\{\alpha\right\}\cup\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)$ consists of the pattern starts that determine all the segments of $\widehat{\bmy}$ which do not appear in $\bmP$ and $\bmS$. Let $\bmA^\prime$ be the sequence of these $r^\prime+1$ segments. Thus we have $\Part_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)=\bmP\bmA^\prime\bmS$.
This proves (ii).

Claim (ii) and \eqref{eq_Fmu} imply that $\bmF_{k}(\bmy)$ is obtained from $\bmF_{k}(\bmx)$ by one $3$-substring edit and $ \left|m_k\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)-m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)\right|\le2$. Since $m_k\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)=m_k\left( \bmx \right)+2$ and $m_k\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)=m_k\left( \bmy \right)+2$, we have $ \left|m_k(\bmx)-m_k(\bmy)\right|\le2$.

To prove (iv), define
\begin{equation}\label{eq_DeltaDN}
 \Delta_D=\sum_{i=1}^{m_{k}(\widehat{\bmx})}D_q\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i \right)-
          \sum_{j=1}^{m_{k}(\widehat{\bmy})}D_q\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right),
 \;
 \Delta_N=\sum_{i=1}^{m_{k}(\widehat{\bmx})}N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i \right)-
          \sum_{j=1}^{m_{k}(\widehat{\bmy})}N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right).
\end{equation}
Let $\cS$ and $\cS^\prime$ be the multisets of segments contained in $\bmA$ and $\bmA^\prime$, respectively. Then by claim (ii), we have $1\le \left|\cS\right|, \left|\cS^\prime\right|\le 3$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq_absDeltaD}
    \begin{aligned}
     \left|\Delta_D\right|&= \left|\sum_{\widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i\in\cS}D_q\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i \right)-
          \sum_{\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j\in\cS^\prime}D_q\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)\right|\\
          &\le\max\left\{\sum_{\widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i\in\cS}D_q\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i \right),\sum_{\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j\in\cS^\prime}D_q\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)\right\}\\
          &\le 3(Q-1)=D_{\max}.
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Notice that no occurrence of $1^k$ overlaps with two segments, because every segment starts with $0^k$. Consequently,
\begin{equation}\label{eq_Nmuwhole}
    \sum_{i=1}^{m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)}N_k\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i \right)
 =N_k\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right),\; \sum_{j=1}^{m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)}N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)=N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right).
\end{equation}
Therefore,
 \begin{equation}\label{eq_DeltaNequiv}
     \Delta_N=N_{k}(\widehat{\bmx})-N_{k}(\widehat{\bmy}).
 \end{equation}
 Suppose that $\widehat{\bmx}_{[a,a+k-1]}=1^{k}$. This occurrence of $1^{k}$ can be destroyed by the $k$-substring edit only if $[a,a+k-1]$ intersects or crosses the edit region in $\widehat{\bmx}$, implying that $\rho-k+2\le a\le \rho+ \left|\bms\right|$. Therefore, there are at most $ \left|\bms\right|+k-1\le 2k-1$ copies of $1^{k}$ in $\widehat{\bmx}$ that can be destroyed. By the same argument, there are at most $2k-1$ copies of $1^{k}$ in $\widehat{\bmy}$ that can be created. Consequently,
 \begin{equation}\label{eq_absDeltaN}
      \left|\Delta_N\right|= \left|N_{k}(\widehat{\bmx})-N_{k}(\widehat{\bmy})\right|\le 2k-1.
 \end{equation}

Now it follows that
\begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
        & \left|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmy)\right)\right|\\
        \le& \left|\sum_{i}D_q\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i \right)-
          \sum_{j}D_q\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)\right|+B \left|\sum_{i}N_k\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i \right)-\sum_{j}N_k\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)\right|+C_{k} \left|\sum_{i} \left|\widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i\right|-\sum_{j} \left|\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j\right|\right|\\
        =& \left|\Delta_D\right|+B \left|\Delta_N\right|+C_{k} \left| \left|\bmx\right|- \left|\bmy\right|\right|\\
        \le& D_{\max}+B(2k-1)+C_{k}k=C_{k}(k+1)-1.
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
This completes the proof of (iv).

Since $C_{k}(k+1)-1<2k C_{k}$, the choice of $C^{(1)}$, $C^{(2)}$ and $C^{(3)}$ in claims (i) and (iv) is correct.

When $\bmx\in\cD_{q,k}(n)$, each segment of $\widehat{\bmx}$ has length at most $\ell_{n,k}$ by \Cref{lem_pdense}. A segment of $\widehat{\bmy}$ contains symbols from at most three segments of $\widehat{\bmx}$, together with at most $k$
symbols of $\bmt$. Its length is therefore at most
$3\ell_{n,k}+k$. This proves~(v).

Finally, claim (vi) follows from \eqref{eq_fmu}, \eqref{eq_Fmu} and (v).
<!-- end ieeeproof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem_patternchange}
    If $m_{k}(\bmx)=m_{k}(\bmy)$, then exactly one of the following two situations occurs.
    \begin{enumerate}[$(i)$]
        \item $\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)=\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)=\varnothing$. In this case, for some
        $i\in[m_k(\widehat{\bmx})]$, the edit region in $\widehat{\bmx}$ is covered by the
        $i$-th segment of $\widehat{\bmx}$ and the edit region in $\widehat{\bmy}$ is covered by
        the $i$-th segment of $\widehat{\bmy}$. Moreover,
        $\widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_j=\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j$ for every
        $j\ne i$.
        \item $ \left|\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)\right|= \left|\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)\right|=1$.
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-10: body_md -->
Set $r= \left|\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)\right|$ and $r^\prime= \left|\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)\right|$.
By \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement} (ii), we have $0\le r,r^\prime\le2$ and $m_k(\bmx)-m_k(\bmy)=m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)-m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)=r-r^\prime$. The assumption $m_k(\bmx)=m_k(\bmy)$ therefore gives $r=r^\prime$.

We exclude the case $r=r^\prime=2$. Suppose first that $r=2$. Write $\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)=\{a_1,a_2\}$ with $a_1<a_2$. It holds that $a_2-a_1\ge 2k$ because both $a_1$ and $a_2$ are pattern starts in $\widehat{\bmx}$. By the definition of $\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)$, both $[a_1,a_1+2k-1]$ and $[a_2,a_2+2k-1]$ intersect or cross the edit region in $\widehat{\bmx}$. If $\bms=\epsilon$, this implies
$a_j\le\rho<a_j+2k-1$ for $j=1,2$, contradicting the fact $a_2-a_1\ge2k$.

Hence $ \left|\bms\right|\ge1$. Put
$L=\rho+1$ and $R=\rho+ \left|\bms\right|$. Since $ \left|\bms\right|\le k$ and both $[a_1,a_1+2k-1]$ and $[a_2,a_2+2k-1]$ intersect $[L,R]$, we have $L\in[a_1+k,a_1+2k-1]$ and $R\in[a_2,a_2+k-1]$. Thus
$s_1=\widehat{\bmx}_L=1$ and $s_{ \left|\bms\right|}=\widehat{\bmx}_R=0$.

The same argument applied to $\widehat{\bmy}$, $\bmt$ and $\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)$ shows that
$r^\prime=2$ implies $ \left|\bmt\right|\ge1$, $t_1=1$, and
$t_{ \left|\bmt\right|}=0$. This contradicts the assumption
$s_1\ne t_1$ and $s_{ \left|\bms\right|}\ne t_{ \left|\bmt\right|}$ in
\eqref{eq_minimalends}.  Hence $r=r^\prime\in\{0,1\}$.

If $r=r^\prime=1$, claim (ii) holds.  If $r=r^\prime=0$, it follows from \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement} (ii) that the edit region in $\widehat{\bmx}$ is covered by the single segment in $\bmA$, and the edit region in $\widehat{\bmy}$ is covered by the single segment in $\bmA^\prime$. \Cref{eq_segmentreplacement} implies that if the segment in $\bmA$ is the $i$-th segment of $\widehat{\bmx}$, then the segment in $\bmA^\prime$ is the $i$-th segment of $\widehat{\bmy}$. Finally, the assertion that $\widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_j=\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j$ for every $j\ne i$ also follows from \eqref{eq_segmentreplacement}.
<!-- end ieeeproof-10 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem_patternshift}
Suppose $ \left|\bmx\right|= \left|\bmy\right|$ and $m_{k}(\bmx)=m_{k}(\bmy)$. If situation (ii) in \Cref{lem_patternchange} holds, then $N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)\ne N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)$.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-11: body_md -->
Since $ \left|\bmx\right|= \left|\bmy\right|$, we have $ \left|\bms\right|= \left|\bmt\right|$. Since $\bmx\ne\bmy$, $1\le  \left|\bms\right|\le k$. Put $[L,R]=[\rho+1,\rho+ \left|\bms\right|]$. Equation~\eqref{eq_minimalends}
gives $\widehat{x}_L\ne\widehat{y}_L$ and
$\widehat{x}_R\ne\widehat{y}_R$.
Write $\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)=\{a\}$ and $\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)=\{b\}$. By definition, it holds that
$\widehat{\bmx}_{[a,a+2k-1]}=\widehat{\bmy}_{[b,b+2k-1]}=\bmp_k$, and both intervals $[a,a+2k-1]$ and $[b,b+2k-1]$ intersect the edit region $[L,R]$.

We first show that $a\ne b$. Suppose $a=b$. Then $\widehat{\bmx}_{[a,a+2k-1]}=\widehat{\bmy}_{[a,a+2k-1]}$. Since $R-L<2k$, the interval $[a,a+2k-1]$ cannot be contained in $[L,R]$. On the other hand, we have $[L,R]\cap[a,a+2k-1]\ne\varnothing$. Therefore, at least one of $L$ and $R$ belongs to $[a,a+2k-1]$, implying $\widehat{x}_L=\widehat{y}_L$ or $\widehat{x}_R=\widehat{y}_R$. This contradicts the fact $\widehat{x}_L\ne\widehat{y}_L$ and $\widehat{x}_R\ne\widehat{y}_R$. Thus $a\ne b$. 

Interchanging $\bmx$ and $\bmy$ interchanges $a$ and $b$. Thus, it is sufficient to assume $a<b$. This arrangement is illustrated in \Cref{fig_patternshift}. We next prove
\begin{equation}\label{eq_twoinclusion}
    L\in[a+k,a+2k-1],\; R\in[b,b+k-1].
\end{equation}
There are three possible relative positions of $a$ and $b$.

If $b\ge a+2k$, \eqref{eq_twoinclusion} follows immediately from the fact that $R-L<k$ and the interval $[L,R]$ intersects both $[a,a+2k-1]$ and $[b,b+2k-1]$.

If $b<a+k$, then $a+k\le b+k-1< a+2k-1$. This gives $\widehat{\bmx}_{[a+k,b+k-1]}=1^{b-a}$ and $\widehat{\bmy}_{[a+k,b+k-1]}=0^{b-a}$, which implies $L\le a+k$ and $R\ge b+k-1$. Since $R-L<k$, $\widehat{\bmx}_{[b,a+k-1]}=\widehat{\bmy}_{[b,a+k-1]}=0^{a+k-b}$, $\widehat{\bmx}_{[b+k,a+2k-1]}=\widehat{\bmy}_{[b+k,a+2k-1]}=1^{a+k-b}$, we conclude that $L=a+k$ and $R=b+k-1$.

Finally, suppose $a+k\le b<a+2k$. Then $a+2k-1\le b+k-1$. Therefore, we have $\widehat{\bmx}_{[b,a+2k-1]}=1^{a+2k-b}$ and $\widehat{\bmy}_{[b,a+2k-1]}=0^{a+2k-b}$, which implies $L\le b$ and $R\ge a+2k-1$. Combining these inequalities with $R-L<k$ yields $a+k\le L\le b$ and $a+2k-1\le R\le b+k-1$. This proves \eqref{eq_twoinclusion} in all three cases.

Combining $L\in[a+k,a+2k-1]$ with $\widehat{x}_L\ne\widehat{y}_L$, we get $\widehat{x}_L=1$ and $\widehat{y}_L\ne1$. Combining $R\in[b,b+k-1]$ with $\widehat{x}_R\ne\widehat{y}_R$, we get $\widehat{x}_R\ne0$ and $\widehat{y}_R=0$. In particular, $\widehat{\bmx}_{[a+k,a+2k-1]}=1^{k}$ but $\widehat{\bmy}_{[a+k,a+2k-1]}\ne 1^{k}$.

It remains to show that $\widehat{\bmy}$ has no occurrence of $1^k$ that is absent from $\widehat{\bmx}$. Consider a length-$k$ substring $\widehat{\bmy}_{[i,i+k-1]}$ of $\widehat{\bmy}$. If $L-k+1\le i\le R$, the interval $[i,i+k-1]$ contains $L$ or $R$. This implies that the substring $\widehat{\bmy}_{[i,i+k-1]}$ must contain a symbol that is not $1$ and thus, $\widehat{\bmy}_{[i,i+k-1]}\ne 1^{k}$. If $i\le L-k$ or $i>R$, we have $\widehat{\bmy}_{[i,i+k-1]}=\widehat{\bmx}_{[i,i+k-1]}$. Therefore, we can conclude that $N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)- N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)\ge1$. This completes the proof.
<!-- end ieeeproof-11 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem_Ddetector}
Let $\bmz,\bmz^\prime\in\Sigma_q^r$ be two distinct sequences. Suppose that there is an interval $[L,R]\subseteq[r]$ with $R-L< k$ such that $z_i\ne z_i^\prime$ only if $i\in [L,R]$. Then $D_q(\bmz)\ne D_q(\bmz^\prime)$.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-12: body_md -->
Because $R-L<k$, the interval $[L,R]$ contains at most one coordinate from each residue class modulo $k$. Then by \eqref{eq_functiond} and the assumption that $\bmz\ne\bmz^\prime$ and $\bmz_{[r]\setminus[L,R]}= \bmz^\prime_{[r]\setminus[L,R]}$, we have $\left( d_1(\bmz),\ldots,d_k(\bmz) \right)\ne \left( d_1(\bmz^\prime),\ldots,d_k(\bmz^\prime) \right)$. Now the conclusion follows from \eqref{eq_functionD}.
<!-- end ieeeproof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem_condition1}
    If $m_{k}(\bmx)=m_{k}(\bmy)$, then $\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\right)\ne\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmy)\right)$.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-13: body_md -->
Let $E=\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmy)\right)$. From \eqref{eq_fmu}, \eqref{eq_Fmu} and \eqref{eq_DeltaDN}, we obtain $E=\Delta_D+B\Delta_N+C_{k}\left(  \left|\widehat{\bmx}\right|- \left|\widehat{\bmy}\right| \right)$. It is sufficient to show $ \left|E\right|>0$.

First suppose $ \left|\bmx\right|\ne \left|\bmy\right|$. By \eqref{eq_extendededit}, we have $ \left| \left|\widehat{\bmx}\right|- \left|\widehat{\bmy}\right|\right|\ge1$. Then $ \left|E\right|\ge C_{k} \left| \left|\widehat{\bmx}\right|- \left|\widehat{\bmy}\right|\right|-\left(  \left|\Delta_D\right|+B \left|\Delta_N\right| \right)\ge C_{k}-\left(  \left|\Delta_D\right|+B \left|\Delta_N\right| \right)\overset{(a)}{\ge} C_{k}-\left( D_{\max}+B(2k-1) \right)>0$, where (a) follows from \eqref{eq_absDeltaD} and \eqref{eq_absDeltaN}, and the last inequality follows from \eqref{eq_TandC}.

Now suppose $ \left|\bmx\right|= \left|\bmy\right|$. If $N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)\ne N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)$, it holds that $ \left|E\right|\ge B \left|\Delta_N\right|- \left|\Delta_D\right|\overset{(b)}{=}B \left|N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)- N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)\right|- \left|\Delta_D\right|\ge B-D_{\max}>0$, where (b) follows from \eqref{eq_DeltaNequiv} and the last inequality follows from \eqref{eq_DandB}.

It remains to consider the case $ \left|\bmx\right|= \left|\bmy\right|$ and $N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)= N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)$. \Cref{lem_patternshift} excludes situation~(ii) of \Cref{lem_patternchange}. Let $i\in\left[ m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right) \right]$ be the index in \Cref{lem_patternchange}~(i). Since $ \left|\widehat{\bmx}\right|= \left|\widehat{\bmy}\right|$ and $\widehat{\bmx}_j^{(k)}=\widehat{\bmy}_j^{(k)}$ for every $j\ne i$, we conclude $ \left|\widehat{\bmx}_i^{(k)}\right|= \left|\widehat{\bmy}_i^{(k)}\right|$. Moreover,  by \Cref{lem_patternchange}~(i), $\widehat{\bmx}_i^{(k)}$ and $\widehat{\bmy}_i^{(k)}$ differ only in the edit regions, whose length is at most $k$. Thus, the hypotheses of \Cref{lem_Ddetector} hold and $ \left|E\right|= \left|\Delta_D\right|= \left|D_q\left( \widehat{\bmx}_i^{(k)} \right)-D_q\left( \widehat{\bmy}_i^{(k)} \right)\right|>0$. This completes the proof.
<!-- end ieeeproof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem_condition2}
    If $m_{k}(\bmx)=m_{k}(\bmy)$, then $\bmF_{k}(\bmx)$ and $\bmF_{k}(\bmy)$ differ in either one coordinate or two consecutive coordinates.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-14: body_md -->
First, it follows from \Cref{lem_condition1} that $\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\ne\bmF_{k}(\bmy)$.

If situation (i) in \Cref{lem_patternchange} holds, then by \eqref{eq_Fmu}, there is some $i\in\left[ m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right) \right]$ such that $\bmF_{k}\left( \bmx \right)_j=\bmF_{k}\left( \bmy \right)_j$ for all $j\ne i$. Since $\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\ne\bmF_{k}(\bmy)$, we conclude that $\bmF_{k}\left( \bmx \right)_{i}\ne\bmF_{k}\left( \bmy \right)_{i}$.

Now suppose that situation (ii) in \Cref{lem_patternchange} holds. By \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement}~(ii), we can write $\bmA=\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i,\widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_{i+1} \right)$ and $\bmA^\prime=\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_i,\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_{i+1} \right)$ for some $1\le i<m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)$. \Cref{eq_Fmu,eq_segmentreplacement} give $\bmF_{k}\left( \bmx \right)_j=\bmF_{k}\left( \bmy \right)_j$ for every $j\notin\{i,i+1\}$. Since $\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\ne\bmF_{k}(\bmy)$, at least one of the two equalities $\bmF_{k}\left( \bmx \right)_{i}=\bmF_{k}\left( \bmy \right)_{i}$ and $\bmF_{k}\left( \bmx \right)_{i+1}=\bmF_{k}\left( \bmy \right)_{i+1}$ must fail. Now the proof is completed.
<!-- end ieeeproof-14 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem_condition3}
  It holds that $ \left|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmy)\right)\right|<\bmF_{k}(\bmy)_i$ for all $1\le i\le m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-15: body_md -->
It follows from \eqref{eq_fmu} and \eqref{eq_Fmu} that $\bmF_{k}(\bmy)_i=f_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_i \right)\ge C_{k} \left|\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_i\right|\ge 2k C_{k}$ for every $i$. On the other hand, \eqref{eq_Fsumdiff} gives $ \left|\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_k(\bmx)\right)-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_k(\bmy)\right)\right|\le C_k(k+1)-1<2kC_k\le \bmF_{k}(\bmy)_i$, completing the proof.
<!-- end ieeeproof-15 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor_locatableverify}
Let $\bmx\in\Sigma_q^n$ and $\bmy$ be obtained from $\bmx$ by one nontrivial $k$-substring edit. Then $\bmF_{k}(\bmy)$ is obtained from $\bmF_{k}(\bmx)$ by a strongly locatable $3$-substring edit.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem_dEDeltaVT}
    Suppose $\bmx\in\cD_{q,k}(n)$.  Given $\bmy$ and the three residues
   $$
       m_k(\bmx)\bmod{5},\quad
       \mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_k(\bmx)\right)\bmod{R_k},\quad
       \mathsf{VT}(\bmF_k(\bmx))\bmod{M_{n,k}},
   $$
one can determine $d$, $E$, and $\Delta_{\rm VT}$ uniquely.
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-16: body_md -->
By \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement}~(iii), $ \left|d\right|\le 2$.  Now the congruence $m_{k}(\bmx)\pmod{5}$ determines $d$ uniquely because $m_{k}(\bmy)$ is known. \Cref{eq_Fsumdiff} gives
$$
 \left|E\right|\le C_k(k+1)-1=\frac{R_k-1}{2}.
$$
Thus knowing $\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}\left( \bmx \right)\right)\pmod{R_{k}}$ is sufficient to obtain the value of $E$.

By \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement}~(iii), the sequence $\bmF_{k}(\bmy)$ is obtained from $\bmF_{k}(\bmx)$ by a $3$-substring edit. Moreover, \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement}~(vi) says that both $\bmF_{k}(\bmx)$ and $\bmF_{k}(\bmy)$ are $A_{n,k}$ bounded. Then it follows from \Cref{lem_absVT} that $ \left|\Delta_{\rm VT}\right|<9\left( \mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\right)+A_{n,k} \right)$. This implies that knowing $\mathsf{VT}\left( \bmF_{k}\left( \bmx \right) \right)\pmod{18\left( \mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\right)+A_{n,k} \right)}$ is sufficient to obtain the value of $\Delta_{\rm VT}$.

It remains to prove $\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\right)\le S_{n,k}$. Since every segment has length at least $2k$ and $ \left|\widehat{\bmx}\right|= \left|\bmx\right|+4k=n+4k$, we have $m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)\le\left\lfloor (n+4k)/(2k) \right\rfloor$. Moreover, it holds that $N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)=1+N_{k}\left( \bmp_{k}\bmx \right)\le n+2$. Then it follows from \eqref{eq_fmu}, \eqref{eq_Fmu} and \eqref{eq_Nmuwhole} that
\begin{align*}
    \mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmF_{k}(\bmx)\right)&=\sum_{i=1}^{m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)}D_q\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i \right)+B\sum_{i=1}^{m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)}N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i \right)+C_{k} \left|\widehat{\bmx}\right|\\
    &\le m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)(Q-1)+B\sum_{i=1}^{m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)}N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i \right)+C_{k}(n+4k)\\
    &=m_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)(Q-1)+BN_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)+C_{k}(n+4k)\\
    &\le\left( \left\lfloor \frac{n}{2k} \right\rfloor+2 \right)(Q-1)+B(n+2)+C_{k}(n+4k)\\
    &=S_{n,k}.
\end{align*}
Now the proof is completed.
<!-- end ieeeproof-16 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem_Gvalue}
If $m_k(\bmx)=m_k(\bmy)$, then $ \left|\bmF_k(\bmx)_i-\bmF_k(\bmy)_i\right|\le \Lambda_{q,k}$ for every
$i\in[m_k(\widehat{\bmx})]$, where
\begin{equation}\label{eq_Lambdaqk}
 \Lambda_{q,k}=(Q-1)+B(4k-2)+C_k(3k-2).
\end{equation}
Consequently, for $r\in\{0,1\}$, the sequence $\bmy$ and the residue $G_r(\bmF_k(\bmx))\bmod(2\Lambda_{q,k}+1)$ determine
$\Delta_{G_r}$ uniquely.
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-17: body_md -->
If $\bmx=\bmy$, every claimed difference is zero. Assume henceforth that the edit is nontrivial. Recall the definition of $\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)$ and $\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)$ from \Cref{def_descrepattern}. Denote $r= \left|\cD_{\rm es}\left( \widehat{\bmx} \right)\right|$ and $r^\prime= \left|\cC_{\rm re}\left( \widehat{\bmy} \right)\right|$. The equality $m_k(\bmx)=m_k(\bmy)$ and
\Cref{lem_patternchange} leave two cases: $r=r^\prime=0$ or $r=r^\prime=1$.

Suppose $r=r^\prime=0$. In this case, there is some
$j\in[m_k(\widehat{\bmx})]$, such that
$\widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i=\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_i$ if and only if $i\ne j$, implying $ \left|\bmF_k(\bmx)_i-\bmF_k(\bmy)_i\right|=0$ for every $i\ne j$. Let $\widehat{\bmx}_{[i_1,i_2]}$ be an occurrence of $1^{k}$ in $\widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_j$. This $1^{k}$ can be destroyed by the $k$-substring edit only if $[i_1,i_2]$ intersects (in the case $ \left|\bms\right|>0$) or crosses (in the case $\bms=\epsilon$) the edit region in $\widehat{\bmx}$. Therefore, there are at most $ \left|\bms\right|+k-1$ occurrences of $1^{k}$ in $\widehat{\bmx}$ that can be destroyed. Similarly, there are at most $ \left|\bmt\right|+k-1$ occurrences of $1^{k}$ in $\widehat{\bmy}$ that can be newly created. Then it follows that $ \left|N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_j \right)-N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)\right|\le\max\left\{ \left|\bms\right|, \left|\bmt\right|\right\}+k-1\le 2k-1$. Now combining \eqref{eq_fmu} and \eqref{eq_Fmu} gives
\begin{align*}
     \left|\bmF_k(\bmx)_j-\bmF_k(\bmy)_j\right|&\le \left|D_q\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_j \right)-D_q\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)\right|+B \left|N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_j \right)-N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)\right|+C_{k} \left| \left|\widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_j\right|- \left|\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j\right|\right|\\
    &\le Q-1+B(2k-1)+C_{k}k<\Lambda_{q,k}.
\end{align*}

Now suppose $r=r^\prime=1$. Write $\cD_{\rm es}(\widehat{\bmx})=\{a\}$ and
$\cC_{\rm re}(\widehat{\bmy})=\{b\}$. Let $\alpha$ and $\beta$ be defined as in the proof of \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement}~(ii). Set $d= \left|\bmt\right|- \left|\bms\right|$. The proof of \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement}~(ii) shows that $\bms$ is contained in the substring $\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ \alpha,a-1 \right]}\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ a,\beta-1 \right]}$, and $\bmt$ is contained in the substring $\widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ \alpha,b-1 \right]}\widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ b,\beta+d-1 \right]}$. Suppose that $\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ \alpha,a-1 \right]}$ is the $j$-th segment of $\widehat{\bmx}$.  \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement}~(ii) claims that $\widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ \alpha,b-1 \right]}$ is the $j$-th segment of $\widehat{\bmy}$. Then it holds that $\widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_i=\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_i$ whenever $i\notin\{j,j+1\}$, yielding $ \left|\bmF_k(\bmx)_i-\bmF_k(\bmy)_i\right|=0$ for every $i\notin\{j,j+1\}$.

To bound $ \left|\bmF_k(\bmx)_j-\bmF_k(\bmy)_j\right|$ and $ \left|\bmF_k(\bmx)_{j+1}-\bmF_k(\bmy)_{j+1}\right|$, we need to bound the four quantities: $ \left| \left|\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ \alpha,a-1 \right]}\right|- \left|\widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ \alpha,b-1 \right]}\right|\right|$, $ \left| \left|\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ a,\beta-1 \right]}\right|- \left|\widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ b,\beta+d-1 \right]}\right|\right|$, $ \left|N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ \alpha,a-1 \right]} \right)-N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ \alpha,b-1 \right]} \right)\right|$ and $ \left|N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ a,\beta-1 \right]} \right)-N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ b,\beta+d-1 \right]} \right)\right|$. 

We first bound $ \left| \left|\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ \alpha,a-1 \right]}\right|- \left|\widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ \alpha,b-1 \right]}\right|\right|$ and $ \left| \left|\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ a,\beta-1 \right]}\right|- \left|\widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ b,\beta+d-1 \right]}\right|\right|$.
To that end, let $\rho= \left|\bmp_{k}\bmu\right|$ (see \eqref{eq_extendededit}). The definition of $\cD_{\rm es}(\widehat{\bmx})$ and $\cC_{\rm re}(\widehat{\bmy})$ gives $\rho-2k+2\le a\le\rho+ \left|\bms\right|$ and $\rho-2k+2\le b\le\rho+ \left|\bmt\right|$, respectively. In other words,
\begin{equation}\label{eq_abinterval}
 a- \left|\bms\right|, b- \left|\bmt\right|\in\left[ \rho-3k+2,\rho \right].
\end{equation}
It is easy to see that
\begin{equation}\label{eq_seglengthdiff1}
     \left| \left|\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ \alpha,a-1 \right]}\right|- \left|\widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ \alpha,b-1 \right]}\right|\right|= \left|a-b\right|\le 3k-2.
\end{equation}
For the second quantity, notice that
\begin{equation}\label{eq_seglengthdiff2}
\begin{aligned}
     \left| \left|\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ a,\beta-1 \right]}\right|- \left|\widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ b,\beta+d-1 \right]}\right|\right|= \left|(\beta+d-b)-(\beta-a)\right|= \left|a+d-b\right|= \left|a- \left|\bms\right|-(b- \left|\bmt\right|)\right|\le 3k-2,
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
where the last inequality follows from \eqref{eq_abinterval}.

Next, we bound $ \left|N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ \alpha,a-1 \right]} \right)-N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ \alpha,b-1 \right]} \right)\right|$ and $ \left|N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ a,\beta-1 \right]} \right)-N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ b,\beta+d-1 \right]} \right)\right|$. Set $\lambda=\min\{a,b,\rho+1\}$. By \eqref{eq_extendededit}, the two segments $\widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ \alpha,a-1 \right]}$ and $\widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ \alpha,b-1 \right]}$
have the common prefix $\widehat{\bmx}_{[\alpha,\lambda-1]}=\widehat{\bmy}_{[\alpha,\lambda-1]}$. After this prefix is removed, their respective lengths are $a-\lambda$ and $b-\lambda$. By the choice of $\lambda$, we have
\begin{align*}
 0\le a-\lambda
 &\le\max\{|a-b|,a-(\rho+1),0\}\le 3k-2,\\
 0\le b-\lambda
 &\le\max\{|a-b|,b-(\rho+1),0\}\le 3k-2,
\end{align*}
where the last inequalities follow from \eqref{eq_abinterval} and the fact $ \left|\bms\right|, \left|\bmt\right|\le k$. An occurrence of $1^{k}$ in $\widehat{\bmx}_{[\alpha,a-1]}$ can be destroyed by the $k$-substring edit only if it overlaps with $\widehat{\bmx}_{[\lambda,a-1]}$, and an occurrence of $1^{k}$ in $\widehat{\bmy}_{[\alpha,b-1]}$ is created by the $k$-substring edit only if it overlaps with $\widehat{\bmy}_{[\lambda,b-1]}$. Thus, it holds that
\begin{equation}\label{eq_Nmudiff1}
     \left|N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ \alpha,a-1 \right]} \right)-N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ \alpha,b-1 \right]} \right)\right|\le\max\left\{a-\lambda,b-\lambda\right\}+k-1<4k-2.
\end{equation}

The two segments $\widehat{\bmx}_{[a,\beta-1]}$ and $\widehat{\bmy}_{[b,\beta+d-1]}$ have the common suffix $\widehat{\bmx}_{[\rho+ \left|\bms\right|+1,\beta-1]}=\widehat{\bmy}_{[\rho+ \left|\bmt\right|+1,\beta+d-1]}$. After this suffix is removed, their respective lengths are $\rho+ \left|\bms\right|-a+1$ and $\rho+ \left|\bmt\right|-b+1$. It follows from \eqref{eq_abinterval} that $\rho+ \left|\bms\right|-a+1,\;\rho+ \left|\bmt\right|-b+1\le 3k-1$. Then by the same argument in the previous paragraph, we have
\begin{equation}\label{eq_Nmudiff2}
     \left|N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}_{\left[ a,\beta-1 \right]} \right)-N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}_{\left[ b,\beta+d-1 \right]} \right)\right|\le 4k-2.
\end{equation}

Now combining \eqref{eq_fmu}, \eqref{eq_Fmu}, \eqref{eq_seglengthdiff1} and \eqref{eq_Nmudiff1} gives
\begin{align*}
     \left|\bmF_k(\bmx)_j-\bmF_k(\bmy)_j\right|&\le \left|D_q\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_j \right)-D_q\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)\right|+B \left|N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_j \right)-N_{k}\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)\right|+C_{k} \left| \left|\widehat{\bmx}^{(k)}_j\right|- \left|\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j\right|\right|\\
    &< Q-1+B(4k-2)+C_{k}(3k-2)=\Lambda_{q,k}.
\end{align*}
Similarly, the bound $ \left|\bmF_k(\bmx)_{j+1}-\bmF_k(\bmy)_{j+1}\right|\le\Lambda_{q,k}$ follows from \eqref{eq_fmu}, \eqref{eq_Fmu}, \eqref{eq_seglengthdiff2} and \eqref{eq_Nmudiff2}.

The preceding argument shows that $ \left|G_r\left( \bmF_{k}(\bmx) \right)-G_r\left( \bmF_{k}(\bmy) \right)\right|\le\Lambda_{q,k}$ for $r\in\{0,1\}$. Then given $\bmy$ and the residue $G_r\left( \bmF_{k}(\bmx) \right)\bmod{2\Lambda_{q,k}+1}$, one can compute the residue $\left( G_r\left( \bmF_{k}(\bmx) \right)-G_r\left( \bmF_{k}(\bmy) \right) \right)\bmod{2\Lambda_{q,k}+1}$ and then obtain the exact value of $G_r\left( \bmF_{k}(\bmx) \right)-G_r\left( \bmF_{k}(\bmy) \right)$. This completes the proof.
<!-- end ieeeproof-17 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm_specificlocator}
Let $n\ge\ell_{n,k}$ be a sufficiently large integer, where $\ell_{n,k}$ is given in \eqref{eq_ellnk}.
Suppose $\bmx\in\cD_{q,k}(n)$ and $\bmx\to_{k}\bmy$. From $\bmy$ and $\mathsf{Loc}_{\rm edit}(\bmx)$, one can first determine whether $\bmy=\bmx$. If $\bmy\ne\bmx$, one can compute an interval in $[n]$ covering the edit region in $\bmx$. The interval has length at most
\begin{equation}\label{eq_specificinterval}
\begin{aligned}
    W_{n,k}&=\max\left\{15\ell_{n,k}+5k,
 \left\lceil\frac{36A_{n,k}}{C_k-\frac{C_k(k+1)-1}{2k}}\right\rceil
 +3(3\ell_{n,k}+k)\right\}+2k\\
 &=O_{q,k}\left( \log n \right),
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
where $C_{k}$ and $A_{n,k}$ are defined in \eqref{eq_TandC} and \eqref{eq_Amu}, respectively. Computing the interval $I$ from $\bmy$ and $\mathsf{Loc}_{\rm edit}(\bmx)$ requires $O_{q,k}\left( n(\log n)^2 \right)$ bit operations.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-18: body_md -->
Let $d,E,\Delta_{\rm VT},\Delta_{G_0},\Delta_{G_1}$ be the differences in \eqref{eq_fivediff}. Write $(s_m,s_{\rm VT},s_{\rm wt},s_0,s_1)
 =\mathsf{Loc}_{\rm edit}(\bmx)$. We divide the process of deciding whether $\bmy=\bmx$ and finding the claimed interval into the four steps below. The complexity of each step is analyzed.
\begin{enumerate}[$(i)$]
\item \textbf{Compute $m_k(\bmy)$, $\bmF_k(\bmy)$, $d$, and $E$.}

Form $\widehat{\bmy}=\bmp_k\bmy\bmp_k$, whose length is at most $n+5k$. Scan $\widehat{\bmy}$ to find all occurrences of $\bmp_k=0^k1^k$ and record the pattern starts. These starts, together with the endpoints $1$ and $ \left|\widehat{\bmy}\right|+1$, determine the segment boundaries needed to compute $\bmF_k(\bmy)$. During the same scan, initialize a counter at zero
and increase it by one whenever an occurrence of
$\bmp_k$ is entirely contained in $\bmy$.
At the end of the scan, this counter equals
$m_k(\bmy)$. 

At each position, testing for an occurrence of $\bmp_k$ compares at most $2k$ symbols and hence costs $O_{q,k}(1)$ bit operations. Recording a pattern start or updating an occurrence counter of $\bmp_k$ costs $O_k(\log n)$ bit operations because every pattern start and every value of the counter is an integer in $[0,n+5k]$. Since there are $O_k(n)$ positions, this scan requires $O_{q,k}(n\log n)$ bit operations.

Let $\bmw=\bmF_k(\bmy)$ and $m= \left|\bmw\right|$.
For each segment $\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j$ used in
\eqref{eq_Fmu}, write
$\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j=v_1\cdots v_{\ell_j}$.
We compute the quantities defining $w_j$ as follows.
First, obtain the length $\ell_j$ from the recorded
segment boundaries by one subtraction. This requires $O_k(\log n)$ bit operations, since these boundaries have $O_k(\log n)$ bits.

Next, initialize a count at zero. Scan $\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j$ to find occurrences of $1^k$. Whenever an occurrence is found, increase the count
by one. At the end of the scan, the count equals
$N_k\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)$. At each position, testing for an occurrence of $1^k$ compares at most $k$ symbols and costs $O_{q,k}(1)$ bit operations.
The count is at most $\ell_j\le n+5k$, so increasing
it by one costs $O_k(\log n)$ bit operations.
Thus computing $N_k\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)$
requires $O_{q,k}(\ell_j\log n)$ bit operations.

To compute the residues in \eqref{eq_functiond},
initialize $k$ integers $e_1,\ldots,e_k$ at zero.
Read $v_1,\ldots,v_{\ell_j}$ in order. When reading $v_i$, replace $e_a$ by $(e_a+v_i)\bmod q$, where
$a=1+((i-1)\bmod k)$. The index $a$ cycles through $1,\ldots,k$. At the end of this scan, $e_a=d_a\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)$ for every $a\in[k]$. Each $e_a$ remains in $[0,q-1]$. Since $q$ and $k$ are fixed, each update costs $O_{q,k}(1)$ bit operations. Consequently, all $k$ residues are obtained using $O_{q,k}(\ell_j)$ bit operations.

There are $m=O_k(n)$ segments used in \eqref{eq_Fmu},
and their total length is at most $n+5k$.
Consequently, computing all the segment lengths,
occurrence counts of $1^k$, and residues requires
$O_{q,k}\left( \left( m+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_j \right)\log n \right) =O_{q,k}(n\log n)$ bit operations.

For each $j$, the computed residues determine $D_q\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)=\sum_{a=1}^{k}e_aq^{a-1}$.
Since $q$ and $k$ are fixed, this calculation costs $O_{q,k}(1)$ bit operations. We then compute $w_j=D_q\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)+B N_k\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)+C_k\ell_j$.
The integers $N_k\left( \widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j \right)$ and
$\ell_j$ have $O_k(\log n)$ bits.
Then computing $w_j$ from these quantities
costs $O_{q,k}(\log n)$ bit operations because $B$ and $C_k$ are fixed. Therefore, computing all $m$ entries of $\bmw$ costs $O_{q,k}(n\log n)$ bit operations.

Since $D_q(\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j)\le Q-1$ and
$N_k(\widehat{\bmy}^{(k)}_j)\le\ell_j$, we have
$0\le\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)\le m(Q-1)+(B+C_k)\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_j\le m(Q-1)+(B+C_k)(n+5k)=O_{q,k}(n)$. Hence every partial sum of $w_1,\ldots,w_m$ has $O_{q,k}(\log n)$ bits.
Computing $\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)$ therefore requires $O_{q,k}(m\log n)=O_{q,k}(n\log n)$ further bit operations.

It remains to recover $d$ and $E$. Compute the least nonnegative residues $u_d=(m_k(\bmy)-s_m)\bmod 5$ and $u_E=(s_{\rm wt}-\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right))\bmod R_k$. By \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement}~(iii), \eqref{eq_Fsumdiff}, and \eqref{eq_fivediff}, the integer $d$ is the unique integer in $[-2,2]$ satisfying $d\equiv u_d\pmod 5$, and $E$ is the unique integer in $[-(R_k-1)/2,(R_k-1)/2]$ satisfying $E\equiv u_E\pmod{R_k}$. Therefore, set $d=u_d$ if $u_d\le2$, and $d=u_d-5$ otherwise. Similarly, set $E=u_E$ if $u_E\le(R_k-1)/2$, and $E=u_E-R_k$ otherwise.

The recovery of $d$ and $E$ uses a fixed number of subtractions, divisions with remainder, and comparisons involving $O_{q,k}(\log n)$-bit integers. It therefore costs $O_{q,k}((\log n)^2)$ bit operations. Together with the preceding scan for pattern starts and the computation of $\bmw$ and $\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)$, step~(i) requires $O_{q,k}(n\log n)$ bit operations in total.

\item \textbf{Determine whether $\bmy=\bmx$.}

If $\bmy=\bmx$, then $d=E=0$. Conversely, if $\bmy\ne\bmx$, then \Cref{cor_locatableverify} and condition~(i) of \Cref{def_locatable} imply $(d,E)\ne(0,0)$. Thus $\bmy=\bmx$ if and only if $d=E=0$. Since $ \left|d\right|\le2$ and $ \left|E\right|\le C_k(k+1)-1$, this test costs $O_{q,k}(1)$ bit operations. If $d=E=0$, the procedure stops. For the remaining steps, assume $(d,E)\ne(0,0)$.

\item \textbf{Recover $\Delta_{\rm VT}$ and, when $d=0$, $\Delta_{G_0}$ and $\Delta_{G_1}$.}

We first compute $\mathsf{VT}(\bmw)=\sum_{j=1}^{m}jw_j$. Step~(i) gives $m=O_k(n)$ and $\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)=O_{q,k}(n)$. Therefore, $0\le\mathsf{VT}(\bmw)\le m\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)=O_{q,k}(n^2)$.
Thus every partial sum of $w_1,2w_2,\ldots,mw_m$ has $O_{q,k}(\log n)$ bits. Each multiplication $jw_j$ costs $O_{q,k}((\log n)^2)$ bit operations, and each addition costs $O_{q,k}(\log n)$ bit operations. There are $m=O_k(n)$ terms, resulting in a total of $O_{q,k}(n(\log n)^2)$ bit operations.

Next, use the given residue
$s_{\rm VT}=\mathsf{VT}(\bmF_k(\bmx))\bmod M_{n,k}$
to compute
$u_{\rm VT}=(s_{\rm VT}-\mathsf{VT}(\bmw))\bmod M_{n,k}$,
where $0\le u_{\rm VT}<M_{n,k}$.
By \eqref{eq_fivediff}, we have
$\Delta_{\rm VT}\equiv u_{\rm VT}\pmod{M_{n,k}}$.
The proof of \Cref{lem_dEDeltaVT} gives
$ \left|\Delta_{\rm VT}\right|
<9(S_{n,k}+A_{n,k})=M_{n,k}/2$.
Since $M_{n,k}$ is even and $\Delta_{\rm VT}$ is
an integer, it follows that
$\Delta_{\rm VT}\in
[-M_{n,k}/2+1,M_{n,k}/2-1]$.
Thus $u_{\rm VT}\ne M_{n,k}/2$, and
\[
 \Delta_{\rm VT}=
 \begin{cases}
 u_{\rm VT},&0\le u_{\rm VT}<M_{n,k}/2,\\
 u_{\rm VT}-M_{n,k},&M_{n,k}/2<u_{\rm VT}<M_{n,k}.
 \end{cases}
\]

Since $0\le s_{\rm VT}<M_{n,k}=O_{q,k}(n)$
and $\mathsf{VT}(\bmw)=O_{q,k}(n^2)$,
all integers used in this computation have
$O_{q,k}(\log n)$ bits.
Computing $u_{\rm VT}$ requires one subtraction
and one reduction modulo $M_{n,k}$, costing
$O_{q,k}((\log n)^2)$ bit operations.
Selecting the corresponding value of
$\Delta_{\rm VT}$ requires one comparison and
at most one further subtraction, costing
$O_{q,k}(\log n)$ bit operations.
Therefore, recovering $\Delta_{\rm VT}$ from
$s_{\rm VT}$ and the computed value $\mathsf{VT}(\bmw)$
requires $O_{q,k}((\log n)^2)$ bit operations.

If $d=0$, compute $G_0(\bmw)$ and $G_1(\bmw)$ by adding $w_j$ to the sum indexed by $j\bmod3$ whenever that residue is $0$ or $1$. Each sum is at most $\mathsf{wt}_{L_1}\left(\bmw\right)=O_{q,k}(n)$, implying that these additions require $O_{q,k}(n\log n)$ bit operations. Since $d=0$ implies $m_k(\bmx)=m_k(\bmy)$, \Cref{lem_Gvalue} applies. For $r\in\{0,1\}$, compute $ u_r=(s_r-G_r(\bmw))\bmod(2\Lambda_{q,k}+1)$.
The bound $ \left|\Delta_{G_r}\right|\le\Lambda_{q,k}$ gives $\Delta_{G_r}=u_r$ when $u_r\le\Lambda_{q,k}$, and $\Delta_{G_r}=u_r-(2\Lambda_{q,k}+1)$ otherwise. Since $q$ and $k$ are fixed, the integer $2\Lambda_{q,k}+1$ is independent of $n$. Each integer $s_r-G_r(\bmw)$ has
$O_{q,k}(\log n)$ bits. Reducing such an integer modulo $2\Lambda_{q,k}+1$ requires $O_{q,k}(\log n)$ bit operations. The subsequent comparisons and subtractions involve only integers with $O_{q,k}(1)$ bits and therefore cost $O_{q,k}(1)$ bit operations. Hence recovering both $\Delta_{G_0}$ and $\Delta_{G_1}$ requires $O_{q,k}(\log n)$ bit operations. If $d\ne0$, these differences are not needed in \Cref{thm_framework}. In either case, step~(iii) requires $O_{q,k}(n(\log n)^2)$ bit operations.

\item \textbf{Compute the claimed interval.}

Apply \Cref{thm_framework} to $\widehat{\bmx}$ and $\widehat{\bmy}$ with the partition and segment function from \Cref{subsec_partitionandf}, and with
$$
 \begin{gathered}
 K=3,\quad A=A_{n,k},\quad L=3\ell_{n,k}+k,\\
 C^{(1)}=C_k,\quad C^{(2)}=2k,\quad
 C^{(3)}=C_k(k+1)-1.
 \end{gathered}
$$
The common segment decompositions in \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement}~(ii) give \Cref{assump1}. Parts~(v) and~(vi) of that lemma give the segment-length bound and the $A$-bounded property, respectively. Strong locatability follows from \Cref{cor_locatableverify}, since step~(ii) has established $\bmy\ne\bmx$. Finally, parts~(i) and~(iv) of \Cref{lem_segmentreplacement} give $w_j\ge C_k\ell_j$, $\ell_j\ge2k$, and $ \left|E\right|\le C_k(k+1)-1$, while
\[
 C^{(1)}-\frac{C^{(3)}}{C^{(2)}}
 =\frac{(k-1)C_k+1}{2k}>0.
\]
Thus the hypotheses of \Cref{thm_framework} are satisfied. The partition of $\widehat{\bmy}$ and $\bmF_k(\bmy)$ were computed in step~(i). The required differences $d$, $E$, $\Delta_{\rm VT}$, $\Delta_{G_0}$ and $\Delta_{G_1}$ were obtained in steps~(i) and~(iii).

The original sequence in this application has length $ \left|\widehat{\bmx}\right|=n+4k$. Therefore, the parameter $\lambda$ in \Cref{thm_framework} is
\[
 \lambda=\left\lceil \log\left( 9(n+5k+1)^2A \right) \right\rceil
 =O_{q,k}(\log n),
\]
where $A=A_{n,k}=O_{q,k}(\log n)$ follows from \eqref{eq_ellnk} and \eqref{eq_Amu}. \Cref{thm_framework} consequently computes an interval $\widehat I=[a,b]\subseteq[n+4k]$ covering the edit region in $\widehat{\bmx}$ using $O_{q,k}((n+4k)\lambda^2)=O_{q,k}(n(\log n)^2)$ bit operations. Substituting the above parameters into \eqref{eq_geninterval}, and using $5L=15\ell_{n,k}+5k$, gives $ \left|\widehat I\right|\le W_{n,k}$.

To obtain an interval in the coordinates of $\bmx$, define $I=[\pi(a),\pi(b)]$ where $\pi(t)=\min\left\{n,\max\left\{1,t-2k\right\}\right\}$. It is easy to verify that $I$ covers the edit region in $\bmx$. Moreover, $\pi$ is non-decreasing and satisfies $\pi(b)-\pi(a)\le b-a$. Therefore, $I\subseteq[n]$ and $ \left|I\right|\le \left|\widehat I\right|\le W_{n,k}$. Computing these endpoints requires $O_k(\log n)$ further bit operations. Since $\ell_{n,k}$ and $A_{n,k}$ are $O_{q,k}(\log n)$ and $C_k-(C_k(k+1)-1)/(2k)$ is a positive constant, \eqref{eq_specificinterval} also gives $W_{n,k}=O_{q,k}(\log n)$.
\end{enumerate}

Steps~(i) and~(ii) cost $O_{q,k}(n\log n)$ and $O_{q,k}(1)$ bit operations, respectively. Each of steps~(iii) and~(iv) costs $O_{q,k}(n(\log n)^2)$ bit operations. Their sum is $O_{q,k}(n(\log n)^2)$. Now the proof is completed.
<!-- end ieeeproof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem_confusionball}
For every $\bmb\in\Sigma_q^m$, it holds that $ \left|\cB_{k}(\bmb)\right|\le D_m$. All members of $\cB_k(\bmb)$ can be generated explicitly in $O_{q,k}(m^3\log m)$ bit operations.
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- ieeeproof-19: body_md -->
Since every $k$-substring edit is reversible, a sequence
$\bmc\in\Sigma_q^m$ belongs to $\cB_k(\bmb)$ if and only if there exists $\bmc_1\in\cE_{1,k}(\bmb)$ such that
$\bmc\in\cE_{1,k}(\bmc_1)$. Thus, we can generate $\cB_k(\bmb)$ by the following three steps: first applying every possible $k$-substring edit to $\bmb$, then applying every possible $k$-substring edit to each resulting sequence $\bmc_1$, and finally retaining only the resulting sequences of length $m$ and removing repeated sequences.

Every $\bmc_1\in\cE_{1,k}(\bmb)$ is obtained by first deleting a substring $\bms$ of length at most $k$, and then inserting a sequence $\bmt\in\Sigma_q^{*}$ of length at most $k$ in the same position. There are at most $S_{q,k}$ choices for $\bmt$ and $k+1$ choices for the length of $\bms$. For each $ \left|\bms\right|$, there are $m+1- \left|\bms\right|$ choices for the positions of $\bms$. Therefore, we have $ \left|\cE_{1,k}(\bmb)\right|\le S_{q,k}\sum_{ \left|\bms\right|=0}^{k}(m- \left|\bms\right|+1)=U_m=O_{q,k}(m)$. Since the length of $\bmc_1$ is at most $m+k$, there are $ \left|\cE_{1,k}(\bmc_1)\right|\le U_{m+k}=O_{q,k}(m)$ possibilities for $\bmc$ once $\bmc_1$ is fixed. Therefore, it holds that $ \left|\cB_{k}(\bmb)\right|\le U_mU_{m+k}=D_m=O_{q,k}(m^2)$.

Next, we analyze the bit operations needed to generate $\cB_k(\bmb)$. Constructing each $\bmc_1$ requires copying its unchanged prefix and suffix and writing the inserted symbols, which takes $O_{q,k}(m)$ bit operations. Similarly, constructing a candidate $\bmc$ from a given $\bmc_1$ takes $O_{q,k}(m+k)=O_{q,k}(m)$ bit operations. Thus, generating all the candidates $\bmc$ costs $D_m\left( O(m)+O(m) \right)=O_{q,k}(m^3)$ bit operations. 

To remove repetitions, we use merge sort to arrange the
retained length-$m$ sequences in lexicographic order. This requires at most $O(D_m\log D_m)=O_{q,k}(m^2\log m)$ string comparisons. Each comparison takes $O(m)$ bit operations because the strings have length $m$. Hence the sorting procedure takes $O_{q,k}(m^3\log m)$ bit operations. We then scan the sorted list and retain one copy of each distinct sequence. Notice that equal sequences are consecutive in the sorted list, implying that removing repeated sequences costs additional $O_{q,k}(m^3)$ bit operations. Therefore, all members of $\cB_k(\bmb)$ can be generated without repetitions in $O_{q,k}(m^3\log m)$ bit operations.
<!-- end ieeeproof-19 -->
